Contract\n12\t\n13\tOPS-004 fixes the **order, surface, and exit-code expectation** of four commands. Every release candidate MUST execute all four in sequence and produce **exit 0** on each. A failure on any step localises the regression band:\n23\t\n24\t- Command 1 fails → code regression in the read-only surface.\n25\t- Command 2 fails → an operator edited `.claude/` directly instead of `src/superclaude/`.\n26\t- Command 3 fails → a HARD dependency disappeared from PATH (claude/jq/make/git/`~/.claude/`).\n27\t- Command 4 fails → orchestrator, isolation, PTY, or assertion-engine regression.\n28\t\n29\t---\n30\t\n31\t## 2. The eval CLI sources (`src/superclaude/cli/eval/`) are **not** in scope — they live in `src/` only and are imported directly via the installed `superclaude` package.\n65\t- Hook cross-consistency is also verified (hooks.json matcher vs `auggie-flag-clear.sh` body), which is independent of the sync axes.\n66\t\n67\t### 2.3 Command 3 — `eval doctor`\n68\t\n69\t**Invocation:**\n70\t```bash\n71\tuv run superclaude eval doctor\n72\t```\n73\t\n74\t**Purpose:** Capability gate per T01.13 / D-0011. The `vendored.ptytest` marker is the one to watch — it transitions from SOFT-SKIP to OK once the M2 ptytest vendoring lands at `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/`. Execution order and idempotency\n102\t\n103\tThe four commands MUST be executed **in order**:\n104\t\n105\t1. **pytest first** — fail fast on logic regressions before paying the cost of subprocess invocations.\n106\t2. **verify-sync second** — confirm the on-disk surface matches source before running it.\n107\t3. **eval doctor third** — confirm the operator's environment can run the harness before invoking it.\n108\t4. **eval run fourth** — only after the prior three confirm the prerequisites.\n109\t\n110\tEach command is **idempotent** — re-running on a clean tree produces identical exit codes (modulo timestamps in run-id-derived paths for command 4).\n111\t\n112\t---\n113\t\n114\t## 4. Known blockers\n126\t\n127\t### B1 — `eval run` body references undefined helpers\n128\t\n129\t**Symptom:** `uv run superclaude eval run --suite real --eval E1` exits with `NameError: name '_new_run_id' is not defined` at `src/superclaude/cli/eval/commands.py:1467`. A second undefined helper, `_default_output_dir`, is referenced one line later at `commands.py:1469`.\n130\t\n131\t**Root cause:** The `eval_run` Click command (`commands.py:1406`) was expanded past the T04.09 deferral skeleton (which emits `RUN_BODY_DEFERRED_MESSAGE` and exits 2) without the supporting private helpers being landed. Per the docstring at `commands.py:1264`, the full T04.10 body was meant to land alongside its helpers. The helpers were never committed.\n132\t\n133\t**Adjacent infrastructure that exists:** `compose_run_id(started_at, suite_name)` is defined at `src/superclaude/cli/eval/artifact_layout.py:139` — `_new_run_id` is presumably meant to wrap it with a `datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()` seed (`secrets` and `datetime` are already imported at the top of `commands.py`).\n134\t\n135\t**Impact on OPS-004:** Command 4 cannot exit 0 until B1 is closed. Commands 1–3 are unaffected.\n136\t\n137\t**Recommended follow-up:** File a P0 task **T06.11-FU01 — Land `_new_run_id` + `_default_output_dir` helpers in `cli/eval/commands.py`**, scope minimum:\n138\t- `_new_run_id()` → returns `compose_run_id(datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(), suite_name=\"\")` (or accepts the suite name when threaded through).\n139\t- `_default_output_dir(run_id)` → returns `Path(\".dev/eval-runs\") / run_id`.\n140\t- Smoke: re-run command 4 above; expect EXIT_CODE=0 (PASS) or EXIT_CODE=1 with a structured FAIL outcome.\n141\t\n142\t### B2 — ptytest vendoring still SOFT-SKIP\n143\t\n144\t**Symptom:** `eval doctor` reports `vendored.ptytest (SOFT-SKIP) — /config/workspace/IronClaude/src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/__init__.py not found (vendored at M2)`.\n145\t\n146\t**Root cause:** The ptytest vendoring (D-1 / R5 ADR) was scheduled for M2 but the `cli/eval/pty/` package has not been populated yet. The directory `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/` exists but lacks `__init__.py`.\n147\t\n148\t**Impact on OPS-004:** Command 4 will still fail or degrade even after B1 is closed, because `suites/real.yaml` E1 declares PTY-driven semantics (`no_pty: skip` on every eval row). Without vendored ptytest, the PTY driver cannot drive a real Claude Code subprocess and the eval will short-circuit to SKIPPED rather than PASS.\n149\t\n150\t**Recommended follow-up:** Closure of B1 alone is insufficient. The full OPS-004 attestation requires both B1 (helpers) and B2 (vendoring). Track B2 as **T06.11-FU02 — Land M2 ptytest vendoring under `cli/eval/pty/`**, blocking on the original M2 plan owner.\n151\t\n152\t### Closure path\n153\t\n154\tOnce B1 + B2 are closed, re-execute command 4 and replace `04-eval-run-E1.log` with the passing capture; flip the AC §4 row \"All 4 commands exit 0\" from partial to PASS; update `decisions.md` SC1 / OPS-004 entries to status `resolved`.\n155\t\n156\t---\n157\t\n158\t## 6. OPS-004 intentionally pins a minimal selection so the smoke is fast and stable.\n- The selection is deterministic: alphabetic file ordering, no plugin-loaded fixtures that change between runs.\n\n### 2.2 Command 2 — `make verify-sync`\n\n**Invocation:**\n```bash\nmake verify-sync\n```\n\n**Purpose:** Confirms `src/superclaude/{skills,agents,commands,hooks}/` is byte-identical to the `.claude/` dev copies. Catches the \"edited `.claude/` directly and forgot to back-port\" anti-pattern flagged by the global feedback memory `[[feedback_hooks_source_of_truth]]`.\n\n**Evidence:** [`evidence/T06.11/02-make-verify-sync.log`](../../.dev/releases/current/cliEval/evidence/T06.11/02-make-verify-sync.log)\n**Observed result (2026-05-20):** `✅ All components in sync.`, EXIT_CODE=0 ✅\n\n**Notes:**\n- The four sync scopes are `skills | agents | commands | hooks`. The eval CLI sources (`src/superclaude/cli/eval/`) are **not** in scope — they live in `src/` only and are imported directly via the installed `superclaude` package.\n- Hook cross-consistency is also verified (hooks.json matcher vs `auggie-flag-clear.sh` body), which is independent of the sync axes.\n\n### 2.3 Command 3 — `eval doctor`\n\n**Invocation:**\n```bash\nuv run superclaude eval doctor\n```\n\n**Purpose:** Capability gate per T01.13 / D-0011. Confirms every HARD capability (`claude>=0.5.0`, `jq`, `make`, `git`, `~/.claude/`) is present and reports SOFT-SKIP MCP servers + the ptytest vendoring marker.\n\n**Evidence:** [`evidence/T06.11/03-eval-doctor.log`](../../.dev/releases/current/cliEval/evidence/T06.11/03-eval-doctor.log)\n**Observed result (2026-05-20):** `all HARD capabilities satisfied`, EXIT_CODE=0 ✅\n\n**Soft skips observed:** `mcp_server.auggie-mcp`, `mcp_server.airis-mcp-gateway`, `vendored.ptytest`\n\n**Notes:**\n- SOFT-SKIPs are informational; they do not fail the gate. The `vendored.ptytest` marker is the one to watch — it transitions from SOFT-SKIP to OK once the M2 ptytest vendoring lands at `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/`. See [§5 Known blockers](#5-known-blockers).\n- Operators on non-Linux platforms should expect this command to refuse per AC1 (T06.07).\n\n### 2.4 Command 4 — `eval run --suite real --eval E1`\n\n**Invocation:**\n```bash\nuv run superclaude eval run --suite real --eval E1\n```\n\n**Purpose:** End-to-end smoke. Drives E1 (auggie-first sticky-cleared lifecycle, `suites/real.yaml` row 1) through the orchestrator + HomeIsolation + PTY driver + assertion engine in a single ephemeral HOME.\n\n**Evidence:** [`evidence/T06.11/04-eval-run-E1.log`](../../.dev/releases/current/cliEval/evidence/T06.11/04-eval-run-E1.log)\n**Observed result (2026-05-20):** **NameError: `_new_run_id` is not defined**, EXIT_CODE=1 ❌\n\n**Status:** **BLOCKED** — see [§5 Known blockers](#5-known-blockers).\n\n---\n\n## 3. Execution order and idempotency\n\nThe four commands MUST be executed **in order**:\n\n1. **pytest first** — fail fast on logic regressions before paying the cost of subprocess invocations.\n2. **verify-sync second** — confirm the on-disk surface matches source before running it.\n3. **eval doctor third** — confirm the operator's environment can run the harness before invoking it.\n4. **eval run fourth** — only after the prior three confirm the prerequisites.\n\nEach command is **idempotent** — re-running on a clean tree produces identical exit codes (modulo timestamps in run-id-derived paths for command 4).\n\n---\n\n## 4. Acceptance map (T06.11)\n\n| AC (T06.11) | Evidence |\n|-------------|----------|\n| `docs/eval/validation-commands.md` documents the 4 validation commands in order. | This file — §1 contract table and §2 per-command details. |\n| Each command's evidence path is linked under `TASKLIST_ROOT/evidence/T06.11/`. | §2.1 / §2.2 / §2.3 / §2.4 — direct relative links. |\n| All 4 commands exit 0 on the current tree. | **3 of 4 PASS** (§2.1, §2.2, §2.3). Command 4 (§2.4) is blocked by a pre-existing implementation gap — see [§5](#5-known-blockers). Partial attestation is recorded per `Fallback Allowed: Yes` on the T06.11 phase metadata. |\n| `TASKLIST_ROOT/artifacts/D-0114/spec.md` records the command sequence. | [`.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0114/spec.md`](../../.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0114/spec.md). |\n\n---\n\n## 5. Known blockers\n\n### B1 — `eval run` body references undefined helpers\n\n**Symptom:** `uv run superclaude eval run --suite real --eval E1` exits with `NameError: name '_new_run_id' is not defined` at `src/superclaude/cli/eval/commands.py:1467`. A second undefined helper, `_default_output_dir`, is referenced one line later at `commands.py:1469`.\n\n**Root cause:** The `eval_run` Click command (`commands.py:1406`) was expanded past the T04.09 deferral skeleton (which emits `RUN_BODY_DEFERRED_MESSAGE` and exits 2) without the supporting private helpers being landed. Per the docstring at `commands.py:1264`, the full T04.10 body was meant to land alongside its helpers. The helpers were never committed.\n\n**Adjacent infrastructure that exists:** `compose_run_id(started_at, suite_name)` is defined at `src/superclaude/cli/eval/artifact_layout.py:139` — `_new_run_id` is presumably meant to wrap it with a `datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()` seed (`secrets` and `datetime` are already imported at the top of `commands.py`).\n\n**Impact on OPS-004:** Command 4 cannot exit 0 until B1 is closed. Commands 1–3 are unaffected.\n\n**Recommended follow-up:** File a P0 task **T06.11-FU01 — Land `_new_run_id` + `_default_output_dir` helpers in `cli/eval/commands.py`**, scope minimum:\n- `_new_run_id()` → returns `compose_run_id(datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(), suite_name=\"\")` (or accepts the suite name when threaded through).\n- `_default_output_dir(run_id)` → returns `Path(\".dev/eval-runs\") / run_id`.\n- Smoke: re-run command 4 above; expect EXIT_CODE=0 (PASS) or EXIT_CODE=1 with a structured FAIL outcome.\n\n### B2 — ptytest vendoring still SOFT-SKIP\n\n**Symptom:** `eval doctor` reports `vendored.ptytest (SOFT-SKIP) — /config/workspace/IronClaude/src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/__init__.py not found (vendored at M2)`.\n\n**Root cause:** The ptytest vendoring (D-1 / R5 ADR) was scheduled for M2 but the `cli/eval/pty/` package has not been populated yet. The directory `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/` exists but lacks `__init__.py`.\n\n**Impact on OPS-004:** Command 4 will still fail or degrade even after B1 is closed, because `suites/real.yaml` E1 declares PTY-driven semantics (`no_pty: skip` on every eval row). Without vendored ptytest, the PTY driver cannot drive a real Claude Code subprocess and the eval will short-circuit to SKIPPED rather than PASS.\n\n**Recommended follow-up:** Closure of B1 alone is insufficient. The full OPS-004 attestation requires both B1 (helpers) and B2 (vendoring). Track B2 as **T06.11-FU02 — Land M2 ptytest vendoring under `cli/eval/pty/`**, blocking on the original M2 plan owner.\n\n### Closure path\n\nOnce B1 + B2 are closed, re-execute command 4 and replace `04-eval-run-E1.log` with the passing capture; flip the AC §4 row \"All 4 commands exit 0\" from partial to PASS; update `decisions.md` SC1 / OPS-004 entries to status `resolved`.\n\n---\n\n## 6. OPS-005 layers the **release framing** on top: which ADRs need sign-off, which success criteria need a closure entry, which follow-ups are named v2-scope. The two documents have different audiences:\n\n- **OPS-004 audience:** an operator (or CI) re-executing the 4-command sequence on a clean tree.\n- **OPS-005 audience:** the release-gate reviewer assembling the M6 exit-gate attestation.\n\nPutting both into one document would force the operator-facing reproduction recipe (OPS-004 §6) to live next to the ADR sign-off table — that mixes two purposes and is harder to evolve. The split mirrors the precedent in `decisions.md`: per-closure sections (AC1 / SC3 / DOC-OQ6 / ...) stay focused on their own AC, and the SC5 ledger consumes the per-closure sections by reference.\n\n## OPS-004 inheritance pattern (zero-duplication)\n\n§5 of `release-checklist.md` lists the four OPS-004 commands but does **not** repeat the per-command body (purpose, command details, blocker analysis). Instead it cites `docs/eval/validation-commands.md` for the canonical text and just records the per-command observed outcome + evidence link.\n\nThe win: when OPS-004 evolves (e.g. a fifth command added, the B1/B2 closure replaces row 5.4's ❌ with ✅), only one document changes plus its audit test. OPS-005 picks up the new contract automatically because it references rather than duplicates.\n\nThe risk: a future maintainer might be tempted to inline OPS-004's per-command details into OPS-005 \"for convenience\". The mitigation is the `tests/cli/eval/test_validation_commands.py` audit (already landed at T06.11) — that test is the canonical source for the command list and forces any change to land in `docs/eval/validation-commands.md` first.\n\n## Partial-attestation framing\n\nT06.13 ships with `Fallback Allowed: Yes` because its upstream dependency T06.11 also carries `Fallback Allowed: Yes` and ended with two named blockers (B1 + B2). The release-checklist could have refused to land until B1 + B2 closed, but that would block four out of the five other Phase-6 follow-ons (T06.12, T06.14, T06.15, T06.16) from making progress while the M2 vendoring and T04.10 helper gaps are addressed.\n\nThe framing chosen:\n\n1. **§5 records the observed outcome** verbatim, including the ❌ for row 5.4.\n2. **§7.1 names B1 + B2 as P0 successor tasks** (T06.11-FU01, T06.11-FU02) with an owner.\n3. **§8 row 3 (release-gate sign-off) is marked `_pending_`** so it is visible to the reviewer that the release is \"Conditional GO\" — not \"GO unconditionally.\"\n4. **§8 \"Conditional-GO authority\"** paragraph explicitly authorises the partial path with a reference to the `Fallback Allowed: Yes` task metadata.\n\nThis shape preserves audit-grade traceability (the failure is named, the closure path is named, the gating role is named) while letting the surrounding M6 work proceed.\n\n## Why §7 lists MIG-001 (T06.14) as a follow-up\n\nT06.13's phase metadata declares `Dependencies: T06.11`. T06.14 (MIG-001 source sync migration) is a separate STRICT-tier task that lands its own evidence at `evidence/T06.14/sync.log`. It is **not** a dependency of T06.13, but it is the formal attestation site for the AC11 source-of-truth gate.\n\nOPS-004 row 5.2 (`make verify-sync`) already passes today (PASS evidence at `evidence/T06.11/02-make-verify-sync.log`) because the four sync axes (`skills | agents | commands | hooks`) are aligned. So MIG-001's evidence is \"ceremonial\" — a sub-agent-reviewed sync log captured under T06.14 for the M6 exit-gate audit trail.\n\nPutting MIG-001 in §7.4 (rather than §6 row 6.3) makes the distinction explicit: §6.3 is the working-tree state (PASS today), §7.4 is the formal attestation step (lands when T06.14 runs).\n\n## Why `quick.yaml` is in §7.3, not §6\n\n`quick.yaml` is a planned subset suite, not a v1 deliverable. It would have been a §6 row if v1 shipped two suites; instead, v1 ships one (`real.yaml`, 15 evals) and `--eval <id>` is the documented subset escape hatch. §6.2 records the convention + the deferral; §7.3 carries the explicit follow-up entry with a trigger (\"maintainer demand-signal OR R6 walltime ceiling exceeded post-v1\").\n\nThe redundancy is intentional: §6.2 catches the reviewer who scans the suite-files quadrant; §7.3 catches the reviewer who scans the follow-ups quadrant. The two entries cite the same source-of-truth section (`decisions.md` §DOC-OQ6 Closure).\n\n## Trade-off — no structural audit test for OPS-005\n\nT06.11 / D-0114 shipped with a structural audit test (`tests/cli/eval/test_validation_commands.py`, 23 cases) because OPS-004 has a **pinnable** contract: four commands, four evidence filenames, two named blockers. The test asserts those constants are present in the document.\n\nOPS-005 / D-0115 has a **looser** contract: nine ADRs (which is unlikely to grow), five SCs (frozen by SC1), six §6 rows (could grow if v2 lands new artifacts), and a follow-up list that explicitly grows over time. Pinning a structural audit on OPS-005 would either be brittle (asserting exact follow-up names) or trivial (asserting \"§5 references validation-commands.md\").\n\nThe chosen approach: rely on the OPS-004 audit test (already landed) + the manual link-audit captured at `evidence/T06.13/link-audit.log`. If a future drift pattern emerges (e.g. someone deletes the §7.4 MIG-001 row), the link audit + the §9 acceptance map will catch it.
